<details id="msg-user-1-2026-05-04-11-07-12-cst" class="message-card" data-role="user" data-search="User Previous context, please first read the context, then please continue:

Please first read&amp;review the code base for DCASE2026 Task4 carefully:
/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline

The the whole system is composed of an USS model, an SC model, and a TSE model.
The input mixture are first processed by the USS model,  which separate the mixture into three foreground/target stems(at most).
Then the separated audios are feed into the SC model, which would classify the audio into one of the 18 foreground classes.
Finally the TSE model would combine the outputs of SC model and USS model, that would be used as guides/quries to extract the target sound events from the mixture.

You should first undestand the whole big graph. Then focus on the USS model for currently.
The USS model separated a mixture into 3 foreground sound events(at most), and the mixture may contain  same sound events in one mixture. And it is also possible no foreground sound event is contained in a mixture, the model is expected not to generate any foreground sound for this situation.
Please carefully review the USS related code according to the official rank metric, identify any potential issues or points need to improve.
Evaluation metric
The ranking metric for this task is class-aware permutation-invariant SDRi (CAPI-SDRi), an extension of SDRi designed to jointly evaluate sound event detection and separation.

SDRi (signal-to-distortion ratio improvement) is a standard metric for source separation, but it only measures waveform-level quality and cannot assess whether the system correctly identifies the class labels. To jointly evaluate detection and separation, DCASE 2025 Task 4 adopted class-aware SDRi (CA-SDRi), in which estimated and reference sources are aligned by their class labels: SDRi is computed for true positives (TP), while false positives (FP) and false negatives (FN) receive a fixed penalty of 0 dB. However, in the DCASE 2026 Task 4 setting where multiple sources of the same class can appear in a mixture, CA-SDRi becomes invalid because label-based alignment is ambiguous.

DCASE 2026 Task 4 therefore uses CAPI-SDRi, which extends CA-SDRi by introducing a permutation-invariant objective to resolve the assignment ambiguity between estimated and reference sources sharing the same class label. When all source labels in a mixture are distinct, CAPI-SDRi reduces to CA-SDRi.

The formal definition of CAPI-SDRi is given in the task description paper. Below is an outline of the formulation.

The per-class metric component Pc¯
 and the mixture-level CAPI-SDRi are defined as:

Pc¯=Nc¯FNPFN+Nc¯FPPFP+maxσ,π∑i=1Nc¯TPSDRi(s^c¯σ(i),sc¯π(i),y),(2)
CAPI-SDRi=1∑c¯∈C∪C^Nc¯∑c¯∈C∪C^Pc¯,(3)
where the SDRi is calculated as

SDRi(s^,s,y)=SDR(s^,s)−SDR(y,s),SDR(s^,s)=10log10(∥s∥2∥s−s^∥2).
Here, the number of true positives (TP), false positives (FP), and false negatives (FN) in class c¯
 is defined as Nc¯TP
, Nc¯FP
, Nc¯FN
, and their sum is defined as Nc¯
. C
 and C^
 denote the sets of unique labels in the reference C
 and estimated C^
, respectively. σ
 and π
 are the possible index permutations for estimated and reference sources, respectively, and they are chosen to maximize SDRi. This addresses the problem of unknown assignment between the estimation and the reference for same-class sources. PFN
 and PFP
 are penalty values for FN and FP, respectively, both set to 0
 dB. y
 is the waveform at the reference channel of Y
. For zero-target mixtures, if an event is detected incorrectly, it will be penalized as FP and the metric is computed normally. When the system correctly predicts silence (i.e., no FP), the metric is undefined since there are no sources to evaluate; such mixtures are excluded from the final averaging. Hence, correctly predicting silence contributes nothing to the score, whereas false predictions are penalized.

The ranking metric is the average CAPI-SDRi across all mixtures.

I’ll inspect the repository structure and key files first, then map the USS→SC→TSE graph and review the USS path against CAPI-SDRi failure modes: same-class overlap, zero-target mixtures, FP/FN penalties, and permutation ambiguity.

I found the repository through the GitHub connector. I’m now pulling the actual source/config files rather than relying on filenames from memory, because the review depends on concrete implementation choices such as slot assignment, thresholds, and loss terms.

Called tool

Called tool
The USS training config is label-queried separation, not a purely blind USS objective: the model receives a concatenated 54-D label vector for three slots and uses it to create FiLM conditioning plus a hard source mask. That matters because at inference the USS→SC→TSE graph may not have oracle slot labels.

Thought for 3m 3s
I reviewed the updated repo path and configs. The codebase now contains two relevant system graphs:

Official-style baseline graph: M2DAT tagger → label-conditioned ResUNet separator. This is implemented by src.models.s5.s5.S5, where the tagger predicts 3 label vectors, then ResUNet30 separates according to those predicted labels. The README describes this as the AT + SS baseline, and the eval config uses M2DAT_4c + ResUNetK.

Updated Kwon/KAIST-like graph: USS → SC → TSE → SC → TSE → SC. This is implemented in src.models.s5.kwo2025.Kwon2025S5: it first runs USS and takes foreground_waveform, classifies those source estimates with SC, forces SC-silenced slots to zero, runs TSE, classifies again, runs TSE again, and returns the final labels/waveforms.

For the rest of this review, I focus on the USS stage, especially ModifiedDeFTUSSMemoryEfficient trained by modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml.

High-level USS status
The USS direction is reasonable and much closer to what DCASE 2026 needs than the old label-query ResUNet baseline. The recommended USS config trains a blind spatial DeFT model with:

n_foreground: 3

n_interference: 2

n_classes: 18

4-channel FOA input

6-second training mixtures

10-second inference chunks

generated mixtures with nevent_range: [0, 3], dupse_rate: 0.5, and dupse_min_angle: 60.0

The USSDataset correctly exposes foreground, interference, noise, class index, silence mask, and activity spans from the generated scene metadata.

The USSLightning path is clean: it feeds only the mixture into the model and builds the target dict separately, so this is a true blind USS training path, unlike the label-query ResUNet baseline.

Critical issues for CAPI-SDRi
1. Local CAPI-SDRi implementation may not match the official objective
Your metric text says the permutation should maximize the sum of SDRi. In s5capi_metric.py, the default S5ClassAwareMetric._pi_metric() computes both metrics and metrics_i, but chooses the best permutation using metrics.mean(...), i.e. raw SDR, not SDRi. There is a separate S5ClassAwareMetricSDRiAssignment class that selects by metrics_i, but evaluate.py instantiates the default S5ClassAwareMetric(metricfunc=&#x27;sdr&#x27;).

This can change validation ranking for same-class mixtures, especially when different references have different mixture-baseline SDR. I would treat this as a must-fix before model selection.

Recommended change: use the SDRi-assignment metric for validation, or at least log both default and SDRi-assignment metrics and select checkpoints by the SDRi-assignment version.

2. USS loss is not directly aligned with CAPI-SDRi assignment
uss_loss.py uses pairwise_sa_sdr_loss() for foreground waveform matching, adds class CE, then runs PIT over fg_pair_total. It does not directly maximize SDRi, and the assignment is driven by waveform SDR + class CE, not the exact CAPI-SDRi objective.

For active sources, raw SDR and SDRi differ by the mixture baseline. If every same-class reference has very similar mixture SDR this may not matter much, but in overlapped scenes it can matter. Since CAPI-SDRi is the ranking metric, I would add an optional pairwise_sdri_loss(pred, target, mixture_ref) and use it for assignment/checkpointing.

Practical improvement:

foreground_pair = -SDRi(pred_slot, ref_source, mixture_ref)
assignment = PIT over foreground_pair + small semantic term
loss_fg_wave = matched foreground_pair mean
Keep SA-SDR as an auxiliary term, but make the validation/promotion loss closer to CAPI-SDRi.

3. Zero-target mixtures are handled, but probably underweighted
The config samples nevent_range: [0, 3], so zero-target scenes are included.

The loss also has a silence mechanism: inactive foreground slots are penalized by inactive_source_energy_loss, and silence_logits is trained by BCE against the PIT-derived active/inactive mask.

However, the inactive foreground waveform penalty is only:

lambda_inactive_foreground: 0.05
For CAPI-SDRi, a false positive on a zero-target mixture receives a 0 dB component, while a correct silence contributes nothing. This means the optimal system should be conservative on zero-target/low-confidence slots. A weak inactive-energy penalty may still allow low-level foreground leakage, which becomes dangerous if SC assigns it a non-silence label.

Recommended changes:

Increase hard-negative sampling of zero-target mixtures beyond the natural 25%.

Tune lambda_inactive_foreground on validation; try 0.1, 0.2, 0.5.

Add a mixture-level foreground count head: count ∈ {0,1,2,3}.

Add a post-processing gate using both silence_logits and waveform RMS/energy.

Optimize thresholds by validation CAPI-SDRi, not by source accuracy.

4. Same-class duplicate handling is still fragile
The data generator supports same-class duplication with dupse_rate: 0.5, dupse_min_angle: 60.0, and max_n_dupse: 2, which is good.

But the model itself has no explicit spatial/DoA slot diversity constraint. ModifiedDeFTUSS produces object slots through object_conv, then class/silence heads from foreground object features.

That means same-class sources can still collapse into one slot, especially when they overlap spectrally. CAPI-SDRi will punish this as one TP plus one FN, even if the combined waveform sounds plausible.

Recommended changes:

Add a DoA/spatial embedding head per foreground slot.

Add a same-class duplicate diversity loss: for same-class active references, assigned slot spatial embeddings should match different reference directions.

Add anti-collapse loss between active foreground slot waveforms or masks, especially for same-class mixtures.

Add AIV/IPD features explicitly to the encoder input, not just raw complex FOA channels.

In validation, separately log CAPI-SDRi for:

distinct-class mixtures,

same-class duplicate mixtures,

zero-target mixtures with FP rate.

5. The main Kwon pipeline can permanently kill duplicate slots too early
In Kwon2025S5.predict_label_separate(), after stage-1 SC, the code computes silent_slots, forces those waveforms to zero, and later ORs the silent mask with stage-2 silence decisions. Once a slot is silenced, TSE cannot recover it.

This is risky for DCASE 2026 because duplicate same-class slots are exactly where SC confidence may be lower. The repo has optional duplicate recall logic, but the default config shown does not enable it.

Recommended change: do not hard-zero uncertain slots immediately. Use three states:

active / uncertain / silence
Only hard-zero slots with very strong silence evidence. Let uncertain slots pass into TSE, but mark them lower confidence for final output. For same-class duplicate recovery, enable the duplicate-recall config by default or implement a validation-calibrated duplicate recovery gate.

6. The current USS config uses the standard uss_loss, not the noisy-label robust loss
You mentioned label noise and impure dry stems. The repo already contains uss_noisy_label_loss.py, with soft class targets, class confidence, uncertainty masks, bad-slot silence weighting, and semantic truncation.

But the recommended modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml still uses src.training.loss.uss_loss, not the noisy-label variant.

Given your dataset concern, this is a major mismatch. If a “Cough” dry source contains Speech, the current supervised waveform loss will push the USS model to reproduce the contaminated target as part of the Cough slot. The class CE also trusts the hard class index. This can teach the model the wrong decomposition.

Recommended changes:

Switch USS training to uss_noisy_label_loss.

Add class_confidence, uncertain_slot_mask, or bad_slot_mask in USSDataset when metadata/source-quality checks detect impure stems.

Use semantic truncation after a warmup.

Downweight waveform loss for suspected contaminated dry stems, not only class loss.

Add mixture-consistency/residual losses so the model can place unexplained extra foreground-like content somewhere without corrupting class slots.

7. Non-foreground branches are probably too weak
The USS model predicts foreground, interference, and noise. The loss includes foreground, interference, noise, silence, KL, and optional residual terms. But the config sets:

lambda_non_foreground: 0.01
lambda_residual: 0.0
lambda_inactive_interference: 0.01
lambda_inactive_noise: 0.01

This makes non-foreground separation almost auxiliary. For CAPI-SDRi, foreground quality matters, but if interference/noise branches are weak, target energy may leak into non-foreground branches or interference may remain in foreground slots. Both lower SDRi.

Recommended changes:

Try lambda_non_foreground: 0.05 or 0.1.

Enable a small residual consistency term, e.g. lambda_residual: 0.01, but monitor whether it encourages target leakage.

Add foreground-vs-interference discrimination loss, especially when interference classes are acoustically close to foreground classes.

Track energy distribution: percentage of active target energy captured by foreground slots vs interference/noise slots.

8. 6-second training vs 10-second evaluation is a distribution mismatch
The lite USS config trains on 6-second scenes but evaluates with 10-second inference chunks.

The recipe notes this explicitly as a memory-efficient path.

This is acceptable for bootstrapping, but for final ranking it can hurt long events, late events, and event-boundary behavior. Since CAPI-SDRi is waveform-level over the whole event/source, inconsistent long-context behavior can directly reduce score.

Recommended changes:

Train 6s first, then fine-tune on 10s with batch size 1.

Add random 6s/8s/10s duration curriculum.

Add temporal activity supervision if using temporal model variants.

Evaluate chunk boundary artifacts explicitly by plotting per-source SDRi for events crossing chunk boundaries.

Things that are good
The current USS direction already includes several important components:

Blind USS path using only mixture as model input.

Three foreground slots matching the maximum number of target events.

Same-class duplication in synthesis with 60° separation.

Explicit interference and noise branches.

Class and silence heads inside USS.

BF16-safe wrapper for STFT/ISTFT and phase normalization, which is important for zero-target and near-silent examples.

Stage-level evaluator that can isolate USS, SC, or TSE.

Priority fix list
I would prioritize fixes in this order:

Fix validation metric assignment: use SDRi-based assignment, not raw-SDR assignment.

Calibrate zero-target suppression: stronger inactive loss, count head, validation-tuned thresholds.

Enable robust/noisy-label USS training: switch or extend uss_noisy_label_loss.

Prevent early irreversible slot killing in Kwon2025S5; use uncertain slots instead of hard silence.

Add spatial duplicate handling: DoA/spatial head, diversity loss, same-class duplicate diagnostics.

Fine-tune USS on 10s after 6s bootstrapping.

Strengthen non-foreground modeling to reduce foreground leakage.

My biggest concern is not the DeFT architecture itself. The main risk is metric alignment and decision calibration: CAPI-SDRi punishes FP/FN and same-class slot mistakes, while the current USS training still behaves mostly like waveform PIT plus auxiliary class/silence supervision.

So please list the needed changes for resolving the biggest concern: metric alignment and decision calibration: CAPI-SDRi punishes FP/FN and same-class slot mistakes, while the current USS training still behaves mostly like waveform PIT plus auxiliary class/silence supervision. 019df0f4-2fb4-7491-a926-6d84d3b09fec" open><summary style="border:1px solid #dce5f3;border-left:8px solid #4f7cff;background:#f7faff;border-radius:18px;padding:22px 24px;margin:14px 0 22px 0;box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(79,124,255,0.08);padding-bottom:14px;"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;"><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;padding:4px 10px;border-radius:999px;background:#e2ebff;color:#234ca8;">USER</span><span style="font-size:26px;font-weight:800;color:#172033;">1. USS loss is not directly aligned with CAPI-SDRi assignment<br>uss_loss.py uses pairwise_sa_sdr_loss() for foreground waveform matching, adds class CE, then runs PIT over fg_pair_total. It does not directly maximize SDRi, and the assignment is driven by waveform SDR + class CE, not the exact CAPI-SDRi objective.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">For active sources, raw SDR and SDRi differ by the mixture baseline. If every same-class reference has very similar mixture SDR this may not matter much, but in overlapped scenes it can matter. Since CAPI-SDRi is the ranking metric, I would add an optional pairwise_sdri_loss(pred, target, mixture_ref) and use it for assignment/checkpointing.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Practical improvement:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">foreground_pair = -SDRi(pred_slot, ref_source, mixture_ref)<br>assignment = PIT over foreground_pair + small semantic term<br>loss_fg_wave = matched foreground_pair mean<br>Keep SA-SDR as an auxiliary term, but make the validation/promotion loss closer to CAPI-SDRi.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">3. Zero-target mixtures are handled, but probably underweighted<br>The config samples nevent_range: [0, 3], so zero-target scenes are included.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The loss also has a silence mechanism: inactive foreground slots are penalized by inactive_source_energy_loss, and silence_logits is trained by BCE against the PIT-derived active/inactive mask.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">However, the inactive foreground waveform penalty is only:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">lambda_inactive_foreground: 0.05<br>For CAPI-SDRi, a false positive on a zero-target mixture receives a 0 dB component, while a correct silence contributes nothing. This means the optimal system should be conservative on zero-target/low-confidence slots. A weak inactive-energy penalty may still allow low-level foreground leakage, which becomes dangerous if SC assigns it a non-silence label.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Recommended changes:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Increase hard-negative sampling of zero-target mixtures beyond the natural 25%.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Tune lambda_inactive_foreground on validation; try 0.1, 0.2, 0.5.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add a mixture-level foreground count head: count ∈ {0,1,2,3}.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add a post-processing gate using both silence_logits and waveform RMS/energy.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Optimize thresholds by validation CAPI-SDRi, not by source accuracy.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">4. Same-class duplicate handling is still fragile<br>The data generator supports same-class duplication with dupse_rate: 0.5, dupse_min_angle: 60.0, and max_n_dupse: 2, which is good.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">But the model itself has no explicit spatial/DoA slot diversity constraint. ModifiedDeFTUSS produces object slots through object_conv, then class/silence heads from foreground object features.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">That means same-class sources can still collapse into one slot, especially when they overlap spectrally. CAPI-SDRi will punish this as one TP plus one FN, even if the combined waveform sounds plausible.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Recommended changes:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add a DoA/spatial embedding head per foreground slot.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add a same-class duplicate diversity loss: for same-class active references, assigned slot spatial embeddings should match different reference directions.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add anti-collapse loss between active foreground slot waveforms or masks, especially for same-class mixtures.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add AIV/IPD features explicitly to the encoder input, not just raw complex FOA channels.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">In validation, separately log CAPI-SDRi for:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">distinct-class mixtures,</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">same-class duplicate mixtures,</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">zero-target mixtures with FP rate.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">5. The main Kwon pipeline can permanently kill duplicate slots too early<br>In Kwon2025S5.predict_label_separate(), after stage-1 SC, the code computes silent_slots, forces those waveforms to zero, and later ORs the silent mask with stage-2 silence decisions. Once a slot is silenced, TSE cannot recover it.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">This is risky for DCASE 2026 because duplicate same-class slots are exactly where SC confidence may be lower. The repo has optional duplicate recall logic, but the default config shown does not enable it.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Recommended change: do not hard-zero uncertain slots immediately. Use three states:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">active / uncertain / silence<br>Only hard-zero slots with very strong silence evidence. Let uncertain slots pass into TSE, but mark them lower confidence for final output. For same-class duplicate recovery, enable the duplicate-recall config by default or implement a validation-calibrated duplicate recovery gate.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">6. The current USS config uses the standard uss_loss, not the noisy-label robust loss<br>You mentioned label noise and impure dry stems. The repo already contains uss_noisy_label_loss.py, with soft class targets, class confidence, uncertainty masks, bad-slot silence weighting, and semantic truncation.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">But the recommended modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml still uses src.training.loss.uss_loss, not the noisy-label variant.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Given your dataset concern, this is a major mismatch. If a “Cough” dry source contains Speech, the current supervised waveform loss will push the USS model to reproduce the contaminated target as part of the Cough slot. The class CE also trusts the hard class index. This can teach the model the wrong decomposition.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Recommended changes:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Switch USS training to uss_noisy_label_loss.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add class_confidence, uncertain_slot_mask, or bad_slot_mask in USSDataset when metadata/source-quality checks detect impure stems.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Use semantic truncation after a warmup.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Downweight waveform loss for suspected contaminated dry stems, not only class loss.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add mixture-consistency/residual losses so the model can place unexplained extra foreground-like content somewhere without corrupting class slots.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">7. Non-foreground branches are probably too weak<br>The USS model predicts foreground, interference, and noise. The loss includes foreground, interference, noise, silence, KL, and optional residual terms. But the config sets:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">lambda_non_foreground: 0.01<br>lambda_residual: 0.0<br>lambda_inactive_interference: 0.01<br>lambda_inactive_noise: 0.01</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">This makes non-foreground separation almost auxiliary. For CAPI-SDRi, foreground quality matters, but if interference/noise branches are weak, target energy may leak into non-foreground branches or interference may remain in foreground slots. Both lower SDRi.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Recommended changes:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Try lambda_non_foreground: 0.05 or 0.1.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Enable a small residual consistency term, e.g. lambda_residual: 0.01, but monitor whether it encourages target leakage.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add foreground-vs-interference discrimination loss, especially when interference classes are acoustically close to foreground classes.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Track energy distribution: percentage of active target energy captured by foreground slots vs interference/noise slots.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">8. 6-second training vs 10-second evaluation is a distribution mismatch<br>The lite USS config trains on 6-second scenes but evaluates with 10-second inference chunks.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The recipe notes this explicitly as a memory-efficient path.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">This is acceptable for bootstrapping, but for final ranking it can hurt long events, late events, and event-boundary behavior. Since CAPI-SDRi is waveform-level over the whole event/source, inconsistent long-context behavior can directly reduce score.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Recommended changes:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Train 6s first, then fine-tune on 10s with batch size 1.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add random 6s/8s/10s duration curriculum.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add temporal activity supervision if using temporal model variants.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Evaluate chunk boundary artifacts explicitly by plotting per-source SDRi for events crossing chunk boundaries.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Things that are good<br>The current USS direction already includes several important components:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Blind USS path using only mixture as model input.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Three foreground slots matching the maximum number of target events.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Same-class duplication in synthesis with 60° separation.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Explicit interference and noise branches.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Class and silence heads inside USS.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">BF16-safe wrapper for STFT/ISTFT and phase normalization, which is important for zero-target and near-silent examples.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Stage-level evaluator that can isolate USS, SC, or TSE.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Priority fix list<br>I would prioritize fixes in this order:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Fix validation metric assignment: use SDRi-based assignment, not raw-SDR assignment.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Calibrate zero-target suppression: stronger inactive loss, count head, validation-tuned thresholds.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Enable robust/noisy-label USS training: switch or extend uss_noisy_label_loss.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Prevent early irreversible slot killing in Kwon2025S5; use uncertain slots instead of hard silence.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Add spatial duplicate handling: DoA/spatial head, diversity loss, same-class duplicate diagnostics.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Fine-tune USS on 10s after 6s bootstrapping.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Strengthen non-foreground modeling to reduce foreground leakage.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">My biggest concern is not the DeFT architecture itself. The main risk is metric alignment and decision calibration: CAPI-SDRi punishes FP/FN and same-class slot mistakes, while the current USS training still behaves mostly like waveform PIT plus auxiliary class/silence supervision.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">So please list the needed changes for resolving the biggest concern: metric alignment and decision calibration: CAPI-SDRi punishes FP/FN and same-class slot mistakes, while the current USS training still behaves mostly like waveform PIT plus auxiliary class/silence supervision.</p></div></div></details>


<meta class="nav-message" data-anchor="msg-assistant-2-2026-05-04-11-07-25-cst-" data-role="assistant" data-label="2. Assistant" data-session="timeline">
<details id="msg-assistant-2-2026-05-04-11-07-25-cst-" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant I’ll refresh the relevant repo and memory context first, then I’ll turn the concern into a concrete change list rather than a broad architecture wish list.

The checkout here has the baseline repo one level down (`dcase2026_task4_baseline/`), so I’m adjusting the search path and checking the exact metric/loss/orchestration surfaces.

I’d split this into two tracks: first make validation tell the truth, then make training/inference optimize the same decisions CAPI-SDRi rewards.

**Needed Changes**

| Priority | Change | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | Make evaluation use SDRi-based permutation assignment everywhere. | Current default metric selects same-class permutations by raw SDR in [s5capi_metric.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/metrics/s5capi_metric.py:110), while CAPI-SDRi should maximize SDRi. `evaluate.py` still instantiates the default metric at [evaluate.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/evaluate.py:18). |
| P0 | Add crafted metric tests for same-class duplicate, FP, FN, and zero-target cases. | Before tuning models, we need proof local validation matches official scoring behavior. Include a case where raw-SDR assignment and SDRi assignment choose different permutations. |
| P0 | Select checkpoints by validation CAPI-SDRi or a close USS proxy, not only `epoch_val/loss`. | The USS config monitors `epoch_val/loss` at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:143). That can reward waveform PIT improvements that do not reduce FP/FN or duplicate-slot mistakes. |
| P1 | Add CAPI/SDRi-aware foreground PIT loss. | Current USS foreground assignment is `pairwise_sa_sdr_loss + class CE` at [uss_loss.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/training/loss/uss_loss.py:112). Add optional pairwise SDRi using mixture reference, then use it for active-source assignment. |
| P1 | Strengthen zero-target and inactive-slot calibration. | The dataset includes zero-event mixtures via `nevent_range: [0, 3]` at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:43), but inactive foreground loss is only `0.05` at line 119. Increase/tune this, add zero-target oversampling, and log zero-target FP rate directly. |
| P1 | Add foreground count prediction: `0/1/2/3`. | CAPI-SDRi heavily punishes wrong source count through FP/FN. A count head gives the system an explicit “how many slots should survive?” decision instead of relying only on independent silence logits. |
| P1 | Replace hard early slot killing with calibrated `active / uncertain / silence`. | `Kwon2025S5` zeros slots after SC silence at [kwo2025.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/s5/kwo2025.py:130), then carries that silence forward at lines 144 and 151. For duplicate sources, uncertain slots should pass into TSE instead of being irrecoverably removed. |
| P1 | Calibrate final thresholds against validation CAPI-SDRi. | Tune silence logits, SC probability, waveform RMS/energy, and count consistency jointly. The objective should be final S5 CAPI-SDRi, not source accuracy alone. |
| P2 | Add same-class duplicate diagnostics and recovery. | Training already synthesizes duplicates with `dupse_rate: 0.5` at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:38), but validation should separately report duplicate recall, duplicate FN rate, and duplicate slot collapse. |
| P2 | Add anti-collapse or spatial diversity loss for duplicate active slots. | CAPI-SDRi punishes merging two same-class events into one slot. Add a slot diversity/spatial cue loss, especially when references share class labels. |
| P2 | Use robust/noisy-label USS training when source labels are impure. | The repo already documents `uss_noisy_label_loss` and quality-aware keys in [noisy_label_robust_uss.md](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/docs/noisy_label_robust_uss.md:54), but the main lite config still uses plain `uss_loss` at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:112). |
| P2 | Add final 10-second alignment/fine-tuning stage. | The current USS trains on 6s scenes but infers with 10s chunks at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:24) and line 109. Final ranking should not rely only on 6s crop behavior. |

The shortest high-impact implementation sequence is:

1. Fix local CAPI-SDRi metric assignment and add tests.
2. Add CAPI-SDRi bucket logging: distinct-class, same-class duplicate, zero-target FP, source-count error.
3. Change checkpoint selection to metric-aware validation.
4. Add calibrated silence/count gating before touching architecture.
5. Add SDRi-aware PIT loss and duplicate anti-collapse as opt-in USS variants.
6. Replace hard S5 slot suppression with uncertainty-aware gating.

That order matters: if the metric and calibration harness are wrong, architecture changes can look better while actually moving away from the rank metric.

Add optional pairwise SDRi using mixture reference, then use it for active-source assignment.</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P1</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Strengthen zero-target and inactive-slot calibration.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">The dataset includes zero-event mixtures via <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">nevent_range: [0, 3]</code> at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:43), but inactive foreground loss is only <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">0.05</code> at line 119. Increase/tune this, add zero-target oversampling, and log zero-target FP rate directly.</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P1</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Add foreground count prediction: <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">0/1/2/3</code>.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">CAPI-SDRi heavily punishes wrong source count through FP/FN. A count head gives the system an explicit “how many slots should survive?” decision instead of relying only on independent silence logits.</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P1</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Replace hard early slot killing with calibrated <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">active / uncertain / silence</code>.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;"><code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">Kwon2025S5</code> zeros slots after SC silence at [kwo2025.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/s5/kwo2025.py:130), then carries that silence forward at lines 144 and 151. For duplicate sources, uncertain slots should pass into TSE instead of being irrecoverably removed.</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P1</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Calibrate final thresholds against validation CAPI-SDRi.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Tune silence logits, SC probability, waveform RMS/energy, and count consistency jointly. The objective should be final S5 CAPI-SDRi, not source accuracy alone.</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P2</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Add same-class duplicate diagnostics and recovery.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Training already synthesizes duplicates with <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">dupse_rate: 0.5</code> at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:38), but validation should separately report duplicate recall, duplicate FN rate, and duplicate slot collapse.</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P2</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Add anti-collapse or spatial diversity loss for duplicate active slots.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">CAPI-SDRi punishes merging two same-class events into one slot. Add a slot diversity/spatial cue loss, especially when references share class labels.</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P2</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Use robust/noisy-label USS training when source labels are impure.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#fafcff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">The repo already documents <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">uss_noisy_label_loss</code> and quality-aware keys in [noisy_label_robust_uss.md](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/docs/noisy_label_robust_uss.md:54), but the main lite config still uses plain <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">uss_loss</code> at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:112).</td></tr><tr><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">P2</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">Add final 10-second alignment/fine-tuning stage.</td><td style="border:1px solid #d6dce8;padding:10px 12px;background:#ffffff;font-size:14px;vertical-align:top;">The current USS trains on 6s scenes but infers with 10s chunks at [modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/separation/modified_deft_uss_lite_6s.yaml:24) and line 109. Final ranking should not rely only on 6s crop behavior.</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The shortest high-impact implementation sequence is:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">1. Fix local CAPI-SDRi metric assignment and add tests.<br>2. Add CAPI-SDRi bucket logging: distinct-class, same-class duplicate, zero-target FP, source-count error.<br>3. Change checkpoint selection to metric-aware validation.<br>4. Add calibrated silence/count gating before touching architecture.<br>5. Add SDRi-aware PIT loss and duplicate anti-collapse as opt-in USS variants.<br>6. Replace hard S5 slot suppression with uncertainty-aware gating.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">That order matters: if the metric and calibration harness are wrong, architecture changes can look better while actually moving away from the rank metric.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">&lt;oai-mem-citation&gt;<br>&lt;citation_entries&gt;<br>MEMORY.md:160-186|note=[used prior DCASE notes on calibration duplicate recovery silence contracts and CAPI metric risk]<br>MEMORY.md:16-34|note=[used rollout ids for robust calibration and duplicate recall context]<br>MEMORY.md:74-78|note=[used prior full Task4 review topic context]<br>&lt;/citation_entries&gt;<br>&lt;rollout_ids&gt;<br>019dc7c2-39c1-7642-8cf1-644014e8e0a6<br>019dc544-fe29-75c3-b814-651dfc4624c2<br>019dc4a7-dd49-73d3-9a38-03d429e8cd62<br>&lt;/rollout_ids&gt;<br>&lt;/oai-mem-citation&gt;</p></div></div></details>

**Specific Metric Changes**

1. In `_pi_metric`, replace assignment selection from raw SDR:

```python
metrics_mean = metrics.mean(...)
best_i = torch.argmax(metrics_mean)
```

with SDR improvement:

```python
metrics_i_mean = metrics_i.mean(...)
best_i = torch.argmax(metrics_i_mean)
```

2. Preserve the old raw-SDR assignment as a diagnostic class, e.g. `S5ClassAwareMetricRawSDRAssignment`, so we can still compare old vs official behavior.

3. Make FP/FN penalty tensors device/dtype safe. Some paths use plain `torch.zeros(...)`; better use `ref_wf.new_zeros(...)` or `est_wf.new_zeros(...)`.

4. Sort `all_labels` in `compute_sample` for deterministic per-class traversal. The mean score is the same, but deterministic diagnostics are nicer.

**Tests To Add Or Update**

1. Default metric uses SDRi assignment when raw SDR and SDRi disagree.

2. `S5ClassAwareMetricRawSDRAssignment` still reproduces old behavior for comparison.

3. Same-class duplicate with unequal counts chooses the SDRi-maximizing subset.

4. FP-only class returns `0.0`.

5. FN-only class returns `0.0`.

6. Correct zero-target silence returns `None`, matching official exclusion from averaging.

7. Mixed zero-target false prediction returns `0.0`, so false positives are penalized.

8. Batch `compute()` ignores only `None` true-silence samples and averages penalized FP/FN samples normally.

I’d keep this slice limited to metric correctness and regression tests. In <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">_pi_metric</code>, replace assignment selection from raw SDR:</p><div class="code-block" style="margin:10px 0 18px 0;border:1px solid #d6dce8;border-radius:14px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 12px;"><span>python</span><button class="copy-btn" type="button" onclick="copyCode(this)">Copy</button></div><pre style="margin:0;background:#0f1720;color:#e8eef6;padding:16px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>metrics_mean = metrics.mean(...)
best_i = torch.argmax(metrics_mean)</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">with SDR improvement:</p><div class="code-block" style="margin:10px 0 18px 0;border:1px solid #d6dce8;border-radius:14px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 12px;"><span>python</span><button class="copy-btn" type="button" onclick="copyCode(this)">Copy</button></div><pre style="margin:0;background:#0f1720;color:#e8eef6;padding:16px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>metrics_i_mean = metrics_i.mean(...)
best_i = torch.argmax(metrics_i_mean)</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">2. Preserve the old raw-SDR assignment as a diagnostic class, e.g. <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">S5ClassAwareMetricRawSDRAssignment</code>, so we can still compare old vs official behavior.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">3. Make FP/FN penalty tensors device/dtype safe. Some paths use plain <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">torch.zeros(...)</code>; better use <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">ref_wf.new_zeros(...)</code> or <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">est_wf.new_zeros(...)</code>.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">4. Sort <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">all_labels</code> in <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">compute_sample</code> for deterministic per-class traversal. The mean score is the same, but deterministic diagnostics are nicer.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Tests To Add Or Update**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">1. Default metric uses SDRi assignment when raw SDR and SDRi disagree.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">2. <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">S5ClassAwareMetricRawSDRAssignment</code> still reproduces old behavior for comparison.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">3. Same-class duplicate with unequal counts chooses the SDRi-maximizing subset.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">4. FP-only class returns <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">0.0</code>.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">5. FN-only class returns <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">0.0</code>.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">6. Correct zero-target silence returns <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">None</code>, matching official exclusion from averaging.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">7. Mixed zero-target false prediction returns <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">0.0</code>, so false positives are penalized.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">8. Batch <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">compute()</code> ignores only <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">None</code> true-silence samples and averages penalized FP/FN samples normally.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">I’d keep this slice limited to metric correctness and regression tests. Checkpoint selection and validation logging are the next layer, but they should come after the local metric is trustworthy.</p></div></div></details>

Yes, you’re right. If `S5ClassAwareMetric` is inherited from the official baseline, we should **not change its default behavior** unless the official evaluator changes. I also checked the public official repo: its metric implementation selects the permutation using raw `metrics.mean(...)`, while still returning `metric_i` as SDRi. The DCASE task page’s formula says permutations are chosen to maximize SDRi, so there is a real code-vs-formula mismatch to track, but leaderboard compatibility should follow the official executable scorer unless organizers clarify otherwise. Sources: official baseline repo/README and metric code, plus the DCASE task metric definition page. [GitHub baseline](https://github.com/nttcslab/dcase2026_task4_baseline), [raw metric file](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/nttcslab/dcase2026_task4_baseline/main/src/evaluation/metrics/s5capi_metric.py), [DCASE metric definition](https://dcase.community/challenge2026/task-spatial-semantic-segmentation-of-sound-scenes).

Concrete test changes I’d add:

1. `S5ClassAwareMetric` matches official raw-SDR assignment in a crafted case.
2. `S5ClassAwareMetricSDRiAssignment` differs in the crafted code-vs-formula case.
3. `S5ClassAwareMetricAssignmentComparison` reports the delta correctly.
4. Zero-target correct silence returns `None`.
5. Zero-target false prediction returns `0.0`.
6. FP-only and FN-only class components return `0.0`.
7. Batch `compute()` excludes `None` true-silence samples but includes FP/FN penalties.

So the first slice should be renamed from **“fix local CAPI-SDRi assignment”** to:

<details id="msg-assistant-10-2026-05-04-11-36-27-cst-" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant I’ll map this as a logging/diagnostics slice, not a training change: where metrics are computed, where per-sample JSON summaries are written, and where dataset labels/counts are available.

Yes. I’d implement this as a **diagnostic layer around the official metric**, not inside the scorer’s assignment logic.

**Bucket Logic**

I’d classify each sample from labels only:

```text
n_ref_active = count(ref labels != silence)
n_est_active = count(est labels != silence)

zero_target:
  n_ref_active == 0

zero_target_fp:
  n_ref_active == 0 and n_est_active &gt; 0

distinct_class:
  n_ref_active &gt; 0 and all active ref labels are unique

same_class_duplicate:
  n_ref_active &gt; 0 and any active ref label count &gt; 1

source_count_error:
  n_ref_active != n_est_active
```

Then aggregate:

| Summary key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `overall.mean` | Existing official CAPI-SDRi mean, excluding true silent `None` samples. |
| `distinct_class.mean` | Mean CAPI-SDRi for nonzero-reference scenes with unique target classes. |
| `same_class_duplicate.mean` | Mean CAPI-SDRi for scenes containing duplicate reference classes. |
| `zero_target.total` | Number of zero-target mixtures. |
| `zero_target.false_positive` | Zero-target scenes where system emitted at least one active label. |
| `zero_target.fp_rate` | `false_positive / total`. |
| `source_count_error.total` | Count of scenes where estimated active source count differs from reference active source count. |
| `source_count_error.rate` | Count error rate over all evaluated scenes. |
| `source_count_error.under_count` | `n_est_active &lt; n_ref_active`. |
| `source_count_error.over_count` | `n_est_active &gt; n_ref_active`. |
| `source_count_error.mean_abs_error` | Mean `abs(n_est_active - n_ref_active)`. |

Important detail: **true zero-target correct silence has CAPI value `None` and must be excluded from CAPI means**, but still counted in `zero_target.total` and source-count diagnostics.

**Recommended Implementation Shape**

1. Add `src/evaluation/metrics/capi_bucket_metric.py`:
   - `active_labels(labels)`
   - `sample_bucket(est_labels, ref_labels)`
   - `summarize_capi_buckets(metric_values, est_labels, ref_labels)`

2. In `evaluate.py`:

3. In `evaluate_stage.py`:

4. Add tests:

This gives us the diagnostics needed for TOP-1 style iteration without changing the official score path.

<details id="msg-assistant-12-2026-05-05-20-59-53-cst-" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant I’ll re-read the current repo state first, including the recent metric/bucket/evaluator changes, then I’ll review it in the code-review sense: concrete bugs and risks first, architecture observations after that.

I see new files for validation breakdown, count-head USS variants, FOA spatial features, and count-gated S5 eval configs. I’m going to read those together with the evaluator/model integration points so the review catches interface mismatches rather than just isolated code style.

There are several opt-in variants now, which is the right pattern for this repo. I’m drilling into the count-gate and CAPI-assignment loss code next, since those are the pieces most likely to affect CAPI-SDRi decisions and zero-target behavior.

I reread the current updated surfaces: validation breakdown, metric/evaluator wiring, count-head USS variants, FOA spatial feature frontend, Kwon S5 count gate, and the new USS loss modes. I did not edit files.

**Findings**

1. **High: source-count error logging is still missing.**
The requested breakdown includes distinct-class, same-class duplicate, zero-target FP, and source-count error. The new `S5ValidationBreakdownMetric` logs buckets and zero-target FP, but it never compares `len(est_active)` against `len(ref_active)`; `ref_active` is computed then unused at [s5_validation_breakdown.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/metrics/s5_validation_breakdown.py:130). Add `source_count_error_rate`, `under_count_rate`, `over_count_rate`, `mean_abs_count_error`, and per-bucket count-error stats.

2. **High: `hard_capi` can fall back to identity assignment when class confidence is poor.**
`hard_capi` passes `valid_pair_mask` into PIT at [uss_loss.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/training/loss/uss_loss.py:264). If no full valid permutation exists, `pit_from_pairwise_loss` falls back to identity at [class_aware_pit.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/training/loss/class_aware_pit.py:110). Early in training this is likely, so the assignment can become arbitrary and harmful. Better fallback: waveform/global PIT for that sample, plus a threshold warmup.

3. **Medium: CAPI-training variants optimize SDRi assignment while the official local scorer is locked to raw-SDR assignment.**
The new loss defaults `capi_use_sdri=True` at [uss_loss.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/training/loss/uss_loss.py:216), and the CAPI configs enable it. Since you confirmed the official baseline scorer uses raw-SDR assignment, these variants should be labeled as paper-SDRi variants or paired with `capi_use_sdri: false` official-scorer variants.

4. **Medium: count gate only uses count=0, so it does not address most source-count errors.**
`Kwon2025S5` uses count logits only to suppress all slots when `P(count=0)` is high at [kwo2025.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/s5/kwo2025.py:160). It ignores predicted counts 1/2/3, so over-counting in nonzero scenes remains mostly governed by slot thresholds. A conservative top-k gate from expected count would align better with CAPI FP/FN penalties.

5. **Medium: new eval configs are not runnable in this checkout without checkpoints.**
Both count-gate eval configs reference missing USS/SC/TSE checkpoints, e.g. [kwo2025_top1_like_lite_uss_foa_count_gate.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/eval_configs/kwo2025_top1_like_lite_uss_foa_count_gate.yaml:21). I checked disk: `modified_deft_uss_lite_6s_count_head.ckpt`, `modified_deft_uss_lite_6s_foa_spatial_features_count_head.ckpt`, `modified_deft_tse_lite_6s.ckpt`, and `m2d_sc_stage2.ckpt` are absent.

6. **Low/Medium: package imports are heavy and make lightweight tests brittle.**
Importing `src.models.deft.uss_count_head` executes [src/models/deft/__init__.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/deft/__init__.py:1), which imports `deft_tse` and requires `torchlibrosa`. In the available parent venv, this blocks the new count/FOA tests even though the tested code does not need TSE.

**Architecture Review**

The direction is good: all new behavior is opt-in through sibling configs, which preserves baseline comparability. The split between official CAPI scorer, paper-definition assignment comparison, and validation breakdown is also the right separation.

The count head is a useful calibration head, but architecturally it is downstream of slot predictions: it sees class logits, active logits, and waveform RMS. That makes it a good count-consistency regularizer, not a strong scene-level detector. For zero-target robustness, consider also feeding pooled mixture/encoder features into the count head.

The FOA spatial frontend is a sensible low-risk addition for duplicate same-class separation. AIV/IPD cues should help slot diversity. But because it replaces the encoder stem, it is not checkpoint-compatible with non-FOA USS checkpoints; keep those configs and checkpoint names very explicit.

The validation breakdown is the most immediately valuable piece, but it needs count-error stats before it becomes a proper decision dashboard for CAPI-SDRi.

**Improvement Plan**

1. Add source-count error metrics and tests to `S5ValidationBreakdownMetric`.
2. Add official-scorer CAPI assignment variants: `soft_official_capi` / `hard_official_capi` or `capi_use_sdri: false` sibling configs.
3. Change `hard_capi` fallback from identity to waveform/global PIT when no valid class-confident permutation exists.
4. Add count-gate modes: `count0_only`, `topk_expected_count`, and `topk_argmax_count`, with thresholds calibrated by validation CAPI.
5. Add a small scene-level count head branch from pooled encoder/mix features.
6. Make `src/models/deft/__init__.py` lazy or minimal so isolated model utilities can be imported without all TSE dependencies.

**Verification**

`py_compile` passed for the touched/new review surfaces. Manual execution passed `tests/test_s5_validation_breakdown.py`. Full new-test execution stopped because `/home/cmj/works/ASS/.venv` lacks `torchlibrosa`, and system Python lacks `pytest`.

Since you confirmed the official baseline scorer uses raw-SDR assignment, these variants should be labeled as paper-SDRi variants or paired with <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">capi_use_sdri: false</code> official-scorer variants.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">4. **Medium: count gate only uses count=0, so it does not address most source-count errors.**<br><code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">Kwon2025S5</code> uses count logits only to suppress all slots when <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">P(count=0)</code> is high at [kwo2025.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/s5/kwo2025.py:160). It ignores predicted counts 1/2/3, so over-counting in nonzero scenes remains mostly governed by slot thresholds. A conservative top-k gate from expected count would align better with CAPI FP/FN penalties.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">5. **Medium: new eval configs are not runnable in this checkout without checkpoints.**<br>Both count-gate eval configs reference missing USS/SC/TSE checkpoints, e.g. [kwo2025_top1_like_lite_uss_foa_count_gate.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/eval_configs/kwo2025_top1_like_lite_uss_foa_count_gate.yaml:21). I checked disk: <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">modified_deft_uss_lite_6s_count_head.ckpt</code>, <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">modified_deft_uss_lite_6s_foa_spatial_features_count_head.ckpt</code>, <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">modified_deft_tse_lite_6s.ckpt</code>, and <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">m2d_sc_stage2.ckpt</code> are absent.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">6. **Low/Medium: package imports are heavy and make lightweight tests brittle.**<br>Importing <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">src.models.deft.uss_count_head</code> executes [src/models/deft/__init__.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/deft/__init__.py:1), which imports <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">deft_tse</code> and requires <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">torchlibrosa</code>. In the available parent venv, this blocks the new count/FOA tests even though the tested code does not need TSE.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Architecture Review**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The direction is good: all new behavior is opt-in through sibling configs, which preserves baseline comparability. The split between official CAPI scorer, paper-definition assignment comparison, and validation breakdown is also the right separation.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The count head is a useful calibration head, but architecturally it is downstream of slot predictions: it sees class logits, active logits, and waveform RMS. That makes it a good count-consistency regularizer, not a strong scene-level detector. For zero-target robustness, consider also feeding pooled mixture/encoder features into the count head.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The FOA spatial frontend is a sensible low-risk addition for duplicate same-class separation. AIV/IPD cues should help slot diversity. But because it replaces the encoder stem, it is not checkpoint-compatible with non-FOA USS checkpoints; keep those configs and checkpoint names very explicit.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The validation breakdown is the most immediately valuable piece, but it needs count-error stats before it becomes a proper decision dashboard for CAPI-SDRi.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Improvement Plan**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">1. Add source-count error metrics and tests to <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">S5ValidationBreakdownMetric</code>.<br>2. Add official-scorer CAPI assignment variants: <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">soft_official_capi</code> / <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">hard_official_capi</code> or <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">capi_use_sdri: false</code> sibling configs.<br>3. Change <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">hard_capi</code> fallback from identity to waveform/global PIT when no valid class-confident permutation exists.<br>4. Add count-gate modes: <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">count0_only</code>, <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">topk_expected_count</code>, and <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">topk_argmax_count</code>, with thresholds calibrated by validation CAPI.<br>5. Add a small scene-level count head branch from pooled encoder/mix features.<br>6. Make <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">src/models/deft/__init__.py</code> lazy or minimal so isolated model utilities can be imported without all TSE dependencies.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Verification**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;"><code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">py_compile</code> passed for the touched/new review surfaces. Manual execution passed <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">tests/test_s5_validation_breakdown.py</code>. Full new-test execution stopped because <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">/home/cmj/works/ASS/.venv</code> lacks <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">torchlibrosa</code>, and system Python lacks <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">pytest</code>.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">&lt;oai-mem-citation&gt;<br>&lt;citation_entries&gt;<br>MEMORY.md:218-227|note=[used prior DCASE Task4 metric and pipeline review context]<br>MEMORY.md:238-242|note=[used prior cautions on metric diagnostics versus pipeline fixes and CAPI assignment]<br>&lt;/citation_entries&gt;<br>&lt;rollout_ids&gt;<br>019dc544-fe29-75c3-b814-651dfc4624c2<br>&lt;/rollout_ids&gt;<br>&lt;/oai-mem-citation&gt;</p></div></div></details>

<details id="msg-assistant-16-2026-05-06-22-47-22-cst-" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant I’ll trace the calibration code end to end first: what waveform it measures, how it normalizes/logs energy, and how that compares to the SC model’s own `predict()` energy. Then I’ll verify inside the Docker worktree if the local environment is needed.

The first important clue is that the calibration script is not measuring waveform RMS dB. It consumes `model.predict(...)[\&quot;energy\&quot;]`, and in the SC model that `energy` is `-logsumexp(plain_logits)`. I’m reading the exact SC `forward/predict` and dataset normalization now to see why the threshold scale drifts that low.

The SC “energy” here is an outlier score, not audio energy: lower/more negative means the classifier is more confident/non-silent, and `predict()` silences when `energy &gt; threshold`. So thresholds like `-20` are plausible in that score space, but they are not comparable to waveform levels like `-4.99` unless that value also came from `output[\&quot;energy\&quot;]`. I’m checking available checkpoints/configs and will run a small distribution probe to compare calibration records to direct SC prediction.

There are no saved calibration outputs in this checkout, and only the upstream M2D weight is present locally, not your fine-tuned SC checkpoint. I can still reproduce the scale behavior on the validation dataset with the configured SC model and inspect the decision rule directly.

I reproduced the configured model without a fine-tuned SC checkpoint: its `output[\&quot;energy\&quot;]` is around `-2.85..-4.28`, and the calibrator returns thresholds around that same range. That means the script and `predict()` are using the same score scale locally. I’m doing one more check to compare this score against actual waveform RMS dB, because the naming is easy to misread.

I read it carefully and ran a Docker-side probe. Short version: **I don’t think this is a hidden waveform-normalization mismatch inside `calibrate_sc_energy_thresholds.py`. The bigger issue is that “energy” here is not audio energy, and the calibration dataset/distribution may not match the S5 deployment slots.**

**What The Code Actually Uses**

`calibrate_sc_energy_thresholds.py` calls:

```python
output = model.predict({&quot;waveform&quot;: waveform})
energy = output[&quot;energy&quot;]
```

at [calibrate_sc_energy_thresholds.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/calibrate_sc_energy_thresholds.py:179).

In the SC model, `energy` is:

```python
energy = -torch.logsumexp(plain_logits, dim=-1)
```

for example at [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:520), [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:665), and [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:696).

So this is **logit energy**, not waveform RMS/dB energy.

The silence decision is:

```python
silence = energy &gt; threshold
```

at [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:704).

That means:

**Why `-19` To `-26` Can Happen**

`ArcMarginProduct` scales cosine logits by `s=32` at [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:11). If a fine-tuned classifier produces a high target logit around `20`, then:

```text
energy = -logsumexp(logits) ~= -20
```

So thresholds around `-20` are plausible for very confident clean positives. They are not comparable to waveform dB.

I probed the available non-finetuned local model in Docker. Its SC `energy` range was around:

```text
SC energy=-logsumexp(logits): -4.74 / -4.73 / -4.28
waveform RMS dB:              -240 / -50.44 / -32.95
```

So waveform RMS and SC energy are clearly different quantities.

**Likely Real Problem**

For `m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml`, training uses estimated slots:

[config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml:16)

but validation/calibration uses clean reference dry slots:

[config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml:39)

So the calibrator may learn thresholds from **clean reference positives**, where correct active predictions have very low energy like `-20`, then you apply them to **USS/TSE estimated slots**, whose energies may be much larger, e.g. `-4.99`. That would silence too many true estimated sources.

**Answer**

No, I do not see different normalization inside the calibration script versus SC `predict()`. The script uses the same `model.predict()` energy used by inference. The mismatch is probably:

1. Confusing logit energy with waveform energy.
2. Calibrating on clean dry validation slots but applying to estimated USS/TSE slots.
3. Possibly using precision-heavy calibration settings that choose very strict thresholds.
4. Energy scale changing heavily after ArcFace fine-tuning.

**Recommended Fix**

Calibrate on the same distribution where thresholds are applied:

The main code bug/design issue is not normalization. It is **calibration distribution mismatch**.

The bigger issue is that “energy” here is not audio energy, and the calibration dataset/distribution may not match the S5 deployment slots.**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**What The Code Actually Uses**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;"><code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">calibrate_sc_energy_thresholds.py</code> calls:</p><div class="code-block" style="margin:10px 0 18px 0;border:1px solid #d6dce8;border-radius:14px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 12px;"><span>python</span><button class="copy-btn" type="button" onclick="copyCode(this)">Copy</button></div><pre style="margin:0;background:#0f1720;color:#e8eef6;padding:16px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>output = model.predict({&quot;waveform&quot;: waveform})
energy = output[&quot;energy&quot;]</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">at [calibrate_sc_energy_thresholds.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/calibrate_sc_energy_thresholds.py:179).</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">In the SC model, <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>energy = -torch.logsumexp(plain_logits, dim=-1)</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">for example at [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:520),
