[m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:696).</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">So this is **logit energy**, not waveform RMS/dB energy.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The silence decision is:</p><div class="code-block" style="margin:10px 0 18px 0;border:1px solid #d6dce8;border-radius:14px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 12px;"><span>python</span><button class="copy-btn" type="button" onclick="copyCode(this)">Copy</button></div><pre style="margin:0;background:#0f1720;color:#e8eef6;padding:16px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>silence = energy &gt; threshold</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">at [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:704).</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">That means:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Why <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">-19</code> To <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">-26</code> Can Happen**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;"><code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">ArcMarginProduct</code> scales cosine logits by <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">s=32</code> at [m2d_sc.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/models/m2dat/m2d_sc.py:11). If a fine-tuned classifier produces a high target logit around <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">20</code>, then:</p><div class="code-block" style="margin:10px 0 18px 0;border:1px solid #d6dce8;border-radius:14px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 12px;"><span>text</span><button class="copy-btn" type="button" onclick="copyCode(this)">Copy</button></div><pre style="margin:0;background:#0f1720;color:#e8eef6;padding:16px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>energy = -logsumexp(logits) ~= -20</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">So thresholds around <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">-20</code> are plausible for very confident clean positives. They are not comparable to waveform dB.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">I probed the available non-finetuned local model in Docker. Its SC <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">energy</code> range was around:</p><div class="code-block" style="margin:10px 0 18px 0;border:1px solid #d6dce8;border-radius:14px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 12px;"><span>text</span><button class="copy-btn" type="button" onclick="copyCode(this)">Copy</button></div><pre style="margin:0;background:#0f1720;color:#e8eef6;padding:16px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>SC energy=-logsumexp(logits): -4.74 / -4.73 / -4.28
waveform RMS dB:              -240 / -50.44 / -32.95</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">So waveform RMS and SC energy are clearly different quantities.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Likely Real Problem**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">For <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml</code>, training uses estimated slots:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">[config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml:16)</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">but validation/calibration uses clean reference dry slots:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">[config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/config/label/m2d_sc_stage3_estimated_strong.yaml:39)</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">So the calibrator may learn thresholds from **clean reference positives**, where correct active predictions have very low energy like <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">-20</code>, then you apply them to **USS/TSE estimated slots**, whose energies may be much larger, e.g. <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">-4.99</code>. That would silence too many true estimated sources.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Answer**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">No, I do not see different normalization inside the calibration script versus SC <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">predict()</code>. The script uses the same <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">model.predict()</code> energy used by inference. The mismatch is probably:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">1. Confusing logit energy with waveform energy.<br>2. Calibrating on clean dry validation slots but applying to estimated USS/TSE slots.<br>3. Possibly using precision-heavy calibration settings that choose very strict thresholds.<br>4. Energy scale changing heavily after ArcFace fine-tuning.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">**Recommended Fix**</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">Calibrate on the same distribution where thresholds are applied:</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The main code bug/design issue is not normalization. It is **calibration distribution mismatch**.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">&lt;oai-mem-citation&gt;<br>&lt;citation_entries&gt;<br>MEMORY.md:160-162|note=[used prior SC calibration context]<br>MEMORY.md:181-181|note=[used prior SC energy and dtype debugging context]<br>&lt;/citation_entries&gt;<br>&lt;rollout_ids&gt;<br>019dcf5f-a664-7961-a061-45a373844d62<br>&lt;/rollout_ids&gt;<br>&lt;/oai-mem-citation&gt;</p></div></div></details>

<meta class="nav-message" data-anchor="msg-assistant-18-2026-05-06-22-59-51-cst-" data-role="assistant" data-label="18. Assistant" data-session="timeline">
<details id="msg-assistant-18-2026-05-06-22-59-51-cst-" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant That result is a strong signal: `-0.5` is a very permissive threshold in this code, so it mostly disables the energy gate. I’m going to connect that back to the exact `energy &gt; threshold` rule and the SC accuracy definitions so we don’t optimize the wrong metric.

Yes, that makes sense, and it points to the real issue.

In this SC code, a slot is silenced when:

```python
energy &gt; threshold
```

where `energy = -logsumexp(plain_logits)`.

So if you set threshold to `-0.5`, almost all normal outputs like `-4.99`, `-10`, `-20` satisfy:

```text
energy &lt;= -0.5
```

and therefore are **kept active**, not silenced.

So `threshold = -0.5` is basically “do not silence unless the classifier is extremely uncertain.” If TOP1 accuracy improves, it means the calibrated thresholds like `-19` to `-26` were over-suppressing true active slots.

The likely diagnosis:

1. The SC classifier is already predicting active class labels reasonably well.
2. The energy threshold calibration is too strict.
3. The energy score is not reliably separating silence from active estimated slots.
4. Your reported TOP1 accuracy probably improves because fewer true active slots are converted to silence.

But be careful: this may improve **active TOP1** while hurting **zero-target FP / silence precision / final CAPI-SDRi**. For DCASE, false positives on zero-target or inactive slots matter.

The most important next check is to compare these metrics for `-0.5` vs calibrated thresholds:

```text
active_source_top1_accuracy
silence_accuracy
source_f1
zero_target_fp_rate
source_count_error_rate
final S5 CAPI-SDRi
```

My current interpretation: the energy threshold is acting more like an over-aggressive rejection gate than a useful silence detector. For now, `-0.5` may be a better default than the calibrated values, but I would not trust TOP1 alone. Calibrate against final validation CAPI or at least source F1 plus zero-target FP, not only per-class threshold F-beta. 019df0f4-2fb4-7491-a926-6d84d3b09fec" open><summary style="border:1px solid #d7eadf;border-left:8px solid #3fa56b;background:#f5fcf7;border-radius:18px;padding:22px 24px;margin:14px 0 22px 0;box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(63,165,107,0.08);padding-bottom:14px;"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;"><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;padding:4px 10px;border-radius:999px;background:#dff5e7;color:#1c6b41;">ASSISTANT</span><span style="font-size:26px;font-weight:800;color:#172033;">18. For DCASE, false positives on zero-target or inactive slots matter.</p><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">The most important next check is to compare these metrics for <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">-0.5</code> vs calibrated thresholds:</p><div class="code-block" style="margin:10px 0 18px 0;border:1px solid #d6dce8;border-radius:14px;overflow:hidden;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.5);"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;background:#1f2937;color:#e5edf7;font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;text-transform:uppercase;padding:9px 12px;"><span>text</span><button class="copy-btn" type="button" onclick="copyCode(this)">Copy</button></div><pre style="margin:0;background:#0f1720;color:#e8eef6;padding:16px 18px;overflow:auto;font-size:14px;line-height:1.6;"><code>active_source_top1_accuracy
silence_accuracy
source_f1
zero_target_fp_rate
source_count_error_rate
final S5 CAPI-SDRi</code></pre></div><p style="margin:0 0 14px 0;">My current interpretation: the energy threshold is acting more like an over-aggressive rejection gate than a useful silence detector. For now, <code style="background:#eef3ff;color:#213b86;border:1px solid #cfdbff;border-radius:6px;padding:1px 6px;font-size:0.92em;font-family:SFMono-Regular,Consolas,'Liberation Mono',Menlo,monospace;">-0.5</code> may be a better default than the calibrated values, but I would not trust TOP1 alone. Calibrate against final validation CAPI or at least source F1 plus zero-target FP, not only per-class threshold F-beta.</p></div></div></details>

With this code, `-0.5` is a very loose threshold:

```python
silence = energy &gt; threshold
```

So with threshold `-0.5`, common values like `-4.99`, `-10`, `-20` are **not silenced**. If `active_source_top1_accuracy`, `silence_accuracy`, and `source_f1` all improve, then the calibrated `-19..-26` thresholds are over-suppressing useful predictions and not buying enough silence correctness.

My diagnosis:

1. **The calibration objective is wrong for your target metric.**
   It optimizes per-class accept/reject F-beta on raw predicted class records, not `active_top1`, `silence_accuracy`, `source_f1`, or final S5 CAPI-SDRi.

2. **The SC energy score is not reliable enough as a silence detector.**
   `energy = -logsumexp(logits)` is classifier confidence, not waveform energy. If the model is confidently wrong or confidently assigns silent slots to some class, this score behaves badly.

3. **The validation distribution is probably mismatched.**
   The calibration script likely uses clean/reference validation slots, while the SC in S5 sees USS/TSE estimated slots. Thresholds learned on clean slots can be far too strict for estimated slots.

4. **`-0.5` is effectively a “disable most energy gating” baseline.**
   Since it improves all three metrics, the raw SC predictions plus other pipeline gates are currently better than the calibrated energy rejection.

What I would do next:

- Replace the calibration script’s objective with a threshold sweep that directly optimizes:
  - `source_f1`
  - `active_source_top1_accuracy`
  - `silence_accuracy`
  - and ideally final S5 CAPI-SDRi
- Add diagnostics showing energy quantiles for:
  - true active correct predictions
  - true active wrong predictions
  - true silence slots
  - estimated-source slots

So yes: your result strongly suggests the problem is not “different normalization” but **bad calibration objective/distribution for this energy score**. 019df0f4-2fb4-7491-a926-6d84d3b09fec" open><summary style="border:1px solid #d7eadf;border-left:8px solid #3fa56b;background:#f5fcf7;border-radius:18px;padding:22px 24px;margin:14px 0 22px 0;box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(63,165,107,0.08);padding-bottom:14px;"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;"><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;padding:4px 10px;border-radius:999px;background:#dff5e7;color:#1c6b41;">ASSISTANT</span><span style="font-size:26px;font-weight:800;color:#172033;">20. <meta class="nav-message" data-anchor="msg-assistant-24-2026-05-06-23-26-04-cst" data-role="assistant" data-label="24. Assistant" data-session="timeline">
<details id="msg-assistant-24-2026-05-06-23-26-04-cst" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant These are diagnostic buckets for checking whether the SC model’s `energy` score separates useful cases from silence / bad cases.

`true_active_correct_predictions`

Slots whose ground-truth label is a real foreground class, not silence, and the SC model predicts that class correctly.

Meaning: “active source, classifier got it right.”

Expected energy behavior: these should ideally have the most confident active energy values. In this code, lower / more negative `energy` usually means stronger classifier confidence.

`true_active_wrong_predictions`

Slots whose ground-truth label is a real foreground class, but the SC model predicts the wrong foreground class.

Meaning: “active source exists, but classifier misclassified it.”

Expected energy behavior: if energy is useful, these should often be less confident than correct active predictions. If their energy overlaps heavily with correct predictions, energy thresholding cannot separate correct vs wrong active slots.

`true_silence_slots`

Slots whose ground-truth target is silence / inactive.

Meaning: “there is no real source in this slot.”

Expected energy behavior: these should have weaker active confidence. If true silence slots have similar or lower energy than active slots, then the SC energy score is not reliable for silence gating.

`estimated_source_slots`

Slots coming from estimated sources, for example USS/TSE outputs, rather than clean/reference source waveforms.

Meaning: “this sample is from the real inference-like distribution.”

This bucket is important because clean validation sources and estimated USS/TSE sources can have very different SC energy distributions. If calibration uses clean sources but inference uses estimated sources, the threshold can be badly mismatched. That may be exactly why thresholds like `-19` to `-26` are harmful while `-0.5` works better. 019df0f4-2fb4-7491-a926-6d84d3b09fec" open><summary style="border:1px solid #d7eadf;border-left:8px solid #3fa56b;background:#f5fcf7;border-radius:18px;padding:22px 24px;margin:14px 0 22px 0;box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(63,165,107,0.08);padding-bottom:14px;"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;"><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;padding:4px 10px;border-radius:999px;background:#dff5e7;color:#1c6b41;">ASSISTANT</span><span style="font-size:26px;font-weight:800;color:#172033;">24. <details id="msg-assistant-26-2026-05-07-23-29-18-cst-" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant I’ll trace how `evaluate_stage.py` builds references/estimates in both modes and where `mix_sdr` is computed. The key suspicion is that `mix_sdr` is averaged only over matched/evaluated source sets, so changing labels or filtering changes the reference population, but I’ll verify it in code.

The first search confirms `mix_sdr` is diagnostic state inside `S5ClassAwareMetric`, not a standalone “mixture quality over the whole validation set.” I’m opening the scorer and stage evaluator around the paths that differ with `--use_oracle_labels`.

`mix_sdr` is different because in this evaluator it is **not a fixed mixture baseline over all reference sources**.

It is only a diagnostic recorded for the source/class pairs that actually enter `_pi_metric()`.

In [s5capi_metric.py](/home/cmj/works/ASS/dcase2026baseline/dcase2026_task4_baseline/src/evaluation/metrics/s5capi_metric.py:74), `mix_sdr` is recorded here:

```python
mix_sdr = metrics_mixture[best_i].mean().item()
```

That happens only when one class has both:

```python
est_wf_1c.shape[0] &gt; 0
ref_wf_1c.shape[0] &gt; 0
```

If a reference source becomes FN, or an estimated source becomes FP, the metric adds a `0` penalty but does **not** record `mix_sdr` for that source/class.

So:

That means these two values:

```text
oracle:     mix_sdr = -1.977
non-oracle: mix_sdr = -3.485
```

do **not** mean the input mixture changed. They mean the scorer averaged `SDR(mixture, reference_source)` over different matched source subsets.

The important identity is still visible:

```text
oracle:     raw_sdr 10.787 - mix_sdr -1.977 = sdri 12.764
non-oracle: raw_sdr  8.570 - mix_sdr -3.485 = sdri 12.055
```

So the diagnostic is internally consistent, but `mix_sdr` is label-assignment-dependent in the current implementation.

For debugging, I would add a separate `reference_mix_sdr` diagnostic computed over **all non-silence reference sources**, independent of estimated labels. That value should be identical between oracle and non-oracle runs on the same dataset/checkpoint. 019df0f4-2fb4-7491-a926-6d84d3b09fec" open><summary style="border:1px solid #d7eadf;border-left:8px solid #3fa56b;background:#f5fcf7;border-radius:18px;padding:22px 24px;margin:14px 0 22px 0;box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(63,165,107,0.08);padding-bottom:14px;"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;"><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;padding:4px 10px;border-radius:999px;background:#dff5e7;color:#1c6b41;">ASSISTANT</span><span style="font-size:26px;font-weight:800;color:#172033;">26. <details id="msg-assistant-30-2026-05-07-23-42-25-cst" class="message-card" data-role="assistant" data-search="Assistant Yes, with the corrected mapping, the reason is still the same: current `mix_sdr` is **not a fixed mixture baseline**. It is computed only over the class-matched TP waveform pairs selected by the CAPI scorer.

Your corrected results mean:

**Without `--use_oracle_labels`:**

```text
CAPI-SDRi: 5.192
raw_sdr: 10.787
mix_sdr: -1.977
sdri: 12.764
```

The USS model uses its own predicted labels/silence decisions. Many bad/missed/wrong slots become FP/FN and receive `0` penalty in CAPI-SDRi. But those FP/FN cases do **not** contribute to `raw_sdr`, `mix_sdr`, or `sdri` diagnostics. So the diagnostics are averaged over the easier subset where predicted labels happened to match references.

That explains:

```text
raw_sdr high: 10.787
mix_sdr less negative: -1.977
CAPI-SDRi low: 5.192
```

**With `--use_oracle_labels`:**

```text
CAPI-SDRi: 12.536
raw_sdr: 8.570
mix_sdr: -3.485
sdri: 12.054
```

Oracle labels force estimated USS slots to be labeled by waveform PIT against references. That converts many label misses into TPs, so CAPI-SDRi improves. But now the diagnostic TP set includes more real/harder sources, not only the easy correctly predicted ones. Therefore `raw_sdr` can go down and `mix_sdr` can become more negative.

So the key interpretation is:

```text
without oracle: diagnostic over easy self-selected matched subset
with oracle: diagnostic over broader oracle-matched active source set
```

The input mixture did not change. The reference subset used for `mix_sdr` changed.

After my added change, rerun both modes and compare `reference_mix_sdr`. That value should be nearly identical between oracle and non-oracle runs, because it is computed over all non-silence reference sources before estimated-label filtering. 019df0f4-2fb4-7491-a926-6d84d3b09fec" open><summary style="border:1px solid #d7eadf;border-left:8px solid #3fa56b;background:#f5fcf7;border-radius:18px;padding:22px 24px;margin:14px 0 22px 0;box-shadow:0 6px 18px rgba(63,165,107,0.08);padding-bottom:14px;"><div style="display:flex;align-items:center;gap:10px;"><span style="font-size:12px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:0.08em;padding:4px 10px;border-radius:999px;background:#dff5e7;color:#1c6b41;">ASSISTANT</span><span style="font-size:26px;font-weight:800;color:#172033;">30.
